# Review log — 0111_fix_proposal-0077-re-derived-plan-10-2-proposal-0077

## Standing context

Changelog (compaction pass 1, 2026-10-30): this pass lifted every FACT, DECISION, WATCHOUT, OPEN, UNVERIFIED, and unclosed DEFERRED from the ledger (spec rounds 1 to 6 plus the second spec.2/spec.4 fix rounds), merging repeated anchor-verification FACTs into one line each. Closed and retired: the SHA-gitRef WATCHOUTs (closed by the gitRef decision), the "removal makes checkpoints unresumable" WATCHOUT (closed by the accepted-failure decision), the `safe.directory` and unbounded-path WATCHOUTs and the rules 5/6 git-config OPEN (closed by the G5 design), the §6.2 "archive validator violation" WATCHOUTs (closed by SPEC-6 Edit (b)), the missing-fill-bound WATCHOUTs (closed by the fill-deadline decision), the `warming → failed` WATCHOUTs at old entries 251/256 (superseded, per spec.2.fix-G1.1), the "bind" collision WATCHOUT (closed by the materialization decision), the derived-Runtime WATCHOUT (closed by the pool-Runtime decision, per its CORRECTS), the rule 3 `git init` on an existing `.git` WATCHOUT (closed by the `.git` removal decision), the depth-default WATCHOUT (closed by the depth decision), the §14.1 "clones" and gitClone-symlink UNVERIFIEDs (closed by G6 and G1), and the capture-copy alternates OPEN (closed by four later security reviewers who confirmed no exposure). Applied the CORRECTS in spec.4.fix-G2.1 and spec.4.fix-design-G2.1, which widen the ancestry-exclusion scope, and the CORRECTS in the second spec.4.fix-G1.1, which removes the phrase "the Runtime the session binds to". The ledger and Retired sections were left untouched, because the task names `## Standing context` as the only section this pass edits. The section runs to about 155 lines, which meets the 200-line target.

### Settled

**Tree validation (rule 2)**
- **Rule 2 cites §13.4:** §14 rule 2 cites the §13.4 path-traversal, path-depth, path-length, and symlink rules by name, keeps only the sub-codes, and says "The other §13.4 archive validators do not apply". [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
- **Rule 2 label:** the bold label is **Tree validation.** and every citer writes "§14 rule 2". [spec.4.fix-G3.1]
- **Cache-served symlinks:** a cache-served source follows the §13.4 symlink rule (the archive-validator bullet, spec/13:762, rather than the :751 path bullet), including the Runtime `allowSymlinks` opt-in, default reject. This is the owner's fail-closed choice. [spec.1.fix-G1.1, spec.2.review-applicability.1]
- **Cold-path symlink bypass is code only:** archiveextract.go sets `AllowSymlinks: true` on the cold gitClone path, no spec text states it, and the summary files it as an out-of-scope defect. [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
- **ValidateTree memo key:** `(url, sha, pathPrefix, allow.AllowSymlinks, allow.WorkspaceRoot)`, because CODE-5 step 1 may extend `upload.RuntimeAllow` until it is no longer comparable. [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]
- **upload.RuntimeAllow:** `{AllowSymlinks bool; WorkspaceRoot string}` (pkg/upload/upload.go). bindprepare.go builds it from the Runtime's ArchivePolicy. [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
- **Materialization vocabulary:** cache-served timing and outcome say "materialization" and never "bind", because 0086's §14.1 **Plan binding.** defines "the plan binds". The SPEC-7 counter row drops "when it binds a plan". [spec.4.fix-G3.1, spec.4.fix-design-G3.1]

**Client visibility**
- **§6.2 Client visibility points at §15.1:** SPEC-6 Edit (b) points the bullet at the `UPLOAD_ARCHIVE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` row, and Edit (a) makes the row's cause clause name every §14 rule 2 refusal. [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
- **No collision on Client visibility:** no approved proposal (0085, 0086, 0108, 0109, 0110) edits the Client visibility bullet, the §15.1 row, or the §26.2 layout bullets. 0085 states that Client visibility is unchanged. [spec.2.review-citations.1]
- **Finalize error mapping:** finalize maps every `*upload.ValidationError` to UPLOAD_ARCHIVE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED (podclaimerror.go). The finalize.go doc comment goes stale, and CODE-5 does not own finalize.go. [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
- **details.reason home:** the §15.1 row is the only enumeration of the reasons. `workspace_size_limit` is already the §4.4.4 probe's `checkpoint.skipped` reason, and the §16.1 extraction-aborted labels exclude it on purpose. [spec.2.review-client-surface.1, spec.4.review-client-surface.1]
- **/start materialization:** concurrent-workspace pools materialize at `/start` (BindReservedSlot → stageWorkspace), and code already maps a ValidationError there to UPLOAD_ARCHIVE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED. [spec.6.review-mechanism.1]

**Repository stores and the warm fill**
- **gitRef is a branch or tag:** registration rejects `^[0-9a-f]{40}$` (SPEC-2; CODE-2 `validateSharedAssets` through `workspaceplan.IsCommitSHA` plus a per-entry CEL rule; TEST-1 item 12). [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
- **Ref resolver:** `gitref.pickSHA` resolves only branch and tag names, and plan resolution short-circuits SHAs through `IsCommitSHA`. [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
- **Fill deadline:** SPEC-2 names "the adapter's fill deadline" and gives no value. CODE-4 step 2.1 `--git-store-fill-timeout` (15m) holds the value. [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
- **Fill failure wording:** SPEC-2 says a failed fill "takes the pod to `failed` on its warm-fill path (§6.2)". A further sentence says that on a preConnect pool the fill runs in `sdk_connecting`, the §6.1 watchdog also bounds it, and the deployer sizes `sdkConnectTimeoutSeconds` to cover it. The edge-case list, Decision 13, CODE-4 step 4, and TEST-1 item 18 follow this wording. [spec.2.fix-G1.1 (both rounds)]
- **preConnect timing:** the controller projects warming → sdk_connecting once the pod is Running. The warm-fill-edge clock runs from pod start (lifecycle.go DecideSDKWarm, sdkwarm.go podRunningFor), so the watchdog counts all fill time in either phase. [spec.2.fix-G1.1, spec.3.review-performance.1]
- **Fill failure reaches failed:** agent pods use RestartPolicy Never and CODE-4 step 4 makes a fill error a fatal startup error, so the pod reaches the existing §6.2 `sdk_connecting → failed` container-termination arm or `warming → failed`. [spec.4.review-reliability.1, spec.6.review-applicability.1]
- **preConnect knob:** preConnect is opt-in and false for every reference runtime. `sdkConnectTimeoutSeconds` has a minimum of 0 and no maximum. [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
- **Startup order:** §4.7.9 places the adapter gRPC connect at step 2 and READY at step 4, so "after connect, before ready" conflicts with nothing. [spec.4.review-fresh.1]
- **Materialization bound:** `maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds` (600s) bounds the rule 3 FetchGitPack calls, so the spec needs no per-RPC deadline. [spec.4.review-reliability.1]
- **Store volume:** `/workspace/shared` has no sizeLimit (volumes.go), so "node-local ephemeral storage bounds the volume" is accurate. [spec.4.review-docs-alignment.1]
- **Scrub survival:** the §5.2 whole-pod scrub removes only `/workspace/slots/*`, so stores survive it. [spec.1.review-mechanism.1]
- **Sidecar mount:** the adapter mounts `/workspace/shared/` read-write, so store immutability rests on the adapter never writing there. [spec.1.review-security.1]
- **Embedded mode:** the embedded runtime is first-party. The embedded rw mount of `/workspace/shared` contradicts the §6.4 EROFS sentence and is filed as an unstaged defect (summary). [spec.4.review-security.1, spec.6.review-security.1]
- **warmup_failure_total:** the metric's error_type enum is closed and has no git-fill value. [spec.1.review-performance.1]

**FetchGitPack and the pack limiter**
- **Pack limiter:** pack production runs under a bare `subsystem.Limiter` (`gateway.gitMirror.maxConcurrentPacks`, default 8, CODE-3 steps 7 and 9) with no breaker and no Subsystem name. Only ValidateTree stays on the Upload Handler gate (SPEC-4 Edit (c)). [spec.1.fix-G3.1]
- **subsystem.Limiter:** usable at its zero value. `Acquire(ctx)` blocks until a slot frees and returns ErrLimiterStopped on cancel, and MaxConcurrent <= 0 means unbounded. [spec.1.fix-G3.1]
- **Upload breaker:** it counts every error inside Do and is shared with the upload HTTP path. [spec.1.review-performance.1]
- **Network I/O ungated:** CloneArchive already runs outside the gate, so Mirror.Ensure and ResolveRef stay ungated. [spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
- **Authorizer = pool Runtime:** §14 rule 1 eligibility and the SPEC-5 FetchGitPack authorizer both name "the Runtime of the pod's pool". CODE-5 step 1 takes the url set from the SandboxTemplate runtimeRef. [spec.4.fix-G1.1 (second round)]
- **Pod id at warm:** the adapter reads its pod id from POD_NAME (Downward API). [spec.4.review-client-surface.1]
- **Derived-to-base fallback absent:** ResolvePool matches runtimeRef exactly, so the §5.1 fallback is unimplemented (filed in the summary defects list). The controller renders `--shared-assets` from the template Runtime's own unmerged list. [spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
- **No durable state:** the staged spec adds no etcd, Postgres, or Redis write, and FetchGitPack needs no fencing. [spec.4.review-performance.1]
- **Tier 3 sizing:** claim rate 30/s, minWarm 1,260 over 5 to 10 hot pools, Upload maxConcurrent 2,000, 5 to 30 replicas. [spec.1.review-performance.1]
- **No §28 row:** FetchGitPack needs no §28 register row, by the ReportPodScrub precedent. The §4.7.1 Adapter → Gateway table is the only RPC enumeration. [spec.6.review-applicability.1, spec.4.review-edit-sites.1]
- **§4.1 subsystems:** §4.1 names four subsystems, and pack production is in none of them, as with the other GatewayControl RPCs. [spec.4.review-performance.1]
- **SA token:** §10.3 validates the projected SA token on every pod → gateway request. [spec.4.review-citations.1]
- **url SSRF parity:** gitClone.url has only the `^https://` check and the egress policy, so reusing it for gitRepository is no weaker. [spec.4.review-security.1]

**Slot repositories (rules 3 to 6)**
- **storeCommit predicate:** rule 3 records `lenny.storeCommit` only when the repository keeps alternates and is not shallow. Rule 6 fetches when the pod has no store for `lenny.gitStoreUrl` or holds it at another commit, then rewrites or removes the key. [spec.1.fix-G4.1]
- **storeCommit traces:** reviewers in rounds 2, 4, and 6 traced the delta, shallow, no-have, same-store, different-store, no-store, and re-restore cases and found them consistent. [spec.2.review-fresh.1, spec.2.review-mechanism.1, spec.6.review-reliability.1]
- **Store commit available:** CODE-4's url map holds `(destPath, resolved_commit)`. [spec.1.fix-G4.1]
- **storeCommit tamper is self-harm:** the key is agent-writable, so do not harden it. [spec.1.fix-design-G4.1, spec.2.review-security.1]
- **Runtime-entry removal:** a restore that has to fetch for a url the Runtime no longer names fails on PERMISSION_DENIED. This is an accepted failure, and the Goal is qualified. [spec.1.fix-G4.1]
- **Shallow-restore ancestry:** a shallow-pack restore of an alternates slot whose refs, reflogs, or index reach an ancestor of `lenny.baseCommit` (amend, reset, branch) fails the resume. This is accepted fail-closed, and rules 5 and 6 and SPEC-5 stay unchanged. Any restore served by the shallow pack is affected, which is broader than a force-pushed store (CORRECTS applied). [spec.4.fix-G2.1, spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]
- **Bind-time-shallow slot:** amending the boundary commit yields a parentless commit and `reset HEAD~1` fails, so the failure above does not apply (reproduced locally). [spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]
- **Restore bytes:** a restore never exceeds the shipped full clone's `.git`. [spec.4.review-performance.1]
- **Rule 3 `.git` removal:** rule 3 removes only a pre-existing `.git` at `path`, without following a symlink, before `git init`. The §13.4 exception stays scoped to rules 5 and 6, CODE-5 step 6 uses `--force`, and TEST-1 item 8 has the case. [spec.4.fix-G2.1 (second round)]
- **Uploads may write `.git`:** nothing rejects a `.git` component, and the cold archive carries `.git`. [spec.4.fix-design-G2.1 (second round)]
- **depth default:** SPEC-3 Edit (a) reads "default full clone except as **Cache-served `gitClone` sources.** rule 3 states", and CODE-5 deletes "Omit for a full clone." from schemas/workspaceplan-v1.json. Those two are the only statements of the default. [spec.4.fix-G2.1 (second round)]
- **Cold depth:** the cold path fetches full history unless `depth` is set. [spec.6.review-docs-alignment.1]
- **§13.4 exception home:** SPEC-4 Edit (a) is the one home for agent-written slot repositories. It requires a `.git`-directory-only walk, openat2 resolution of the git dir and objects, and fd-relative writes. It also requires a per-operation adapter git dir and config, with `lenny.*` keys read as data under a 40-hex check. Rules 5 and 6 point at it, and CODE-6 has an intro with four bullets. [spec.1.fix-G5.1]
- **Config isolation:** git runs with a scratch GIT_DIR (copies of HEAD, packed-refs, refs/, logs/, index, shallow, worktrees/) and GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY set to the slot objects. [spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]
- **Scrub resolver:** `openDir` and `openChildDir` are package-private in pkg/adapter/scrub/resolve_linux.go and fail closed off Linux. CODE-6 exports or moves them. [spec.1.fix-G5.1]
- **Staging dir:** `/workspace/staging` is 0700 on the same volume as the slots, so renameat into a slot works. [spec.1.fix-G5.1]
- **CODE-6 walk:** step 2.1 walks only `.git` directories, and step 1 cites it. [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]
- **Immutability sentence:** the SPEC-2 sentence needs no edit. [spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]
- **Co-tenancy:** concurrent slots share the agent UID and are tenant-pinned, so alternates reads during the capture copy expose nothing new. [spec.2.review-mechanism.1, spec.4.review-security.1, spec.6.review-security.1]

### Traps

- **Do not restore an unconditional symlink admission:** do not restore "Symlinks are admitted whatever the Runtime's allowSymlinks setting" for cache-served sources, even without its "as for every gitClone source" clause. That option bypassed a security opt-in with no spec basis, and it made a plan's outcome depend on whether the Runtime holds a store. An implementor of ValidateTree will copy extractGitCloneSource's `AllowSymlinks: true`; CODE-3 step 4 carries this trap. [spec.1.fix-G1.1, spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
- **ValidateTree outside gatedExtract:** `gatedExtract` records `lenny_upload_extraction_aborted_total` on every failure, which contradicts SPEC-6. Call `UploadGate.Do` directly. [spec.1.fix-G3.1]
- **Pack limiter is not a Subsystem:** do not model it as a `subsystem.Subsystem` or publish it through the gauge publisher, because that adds a `subsystem` label outside the §16.1 closed set. The `token_service` Subsystem is a precedent not to copy. The rejected options were a fifth §4.1 subsystem with a breaker (it cascades into §4.1, §16.1, the alert, and §17.8, and one dead remote fails every url), no bound (OOM), and the per-url lock as the bound. [spec.1.fix-G3.1, spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
- **gitRef and gitClone.ref diverge on purpose:** do not align §14 `gitClone.ref` "(branch, tag, or commit SHA)" with gitRepository. The session path pins a SHA through `resolvedCommitSha` with haves, while a store fill sends no haves, so a SHA want gets a shallow pack. The keyed-on-store-fill and shallow-store options were rejected. [spec.1.fix-G2.1, spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
- **The CEL rule is the only CRD enforcer:** `applyCRDFields` bypasses the admin validator, so dropping the CEL rule from CODE-2 item 4 reopens the hole. [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
- **Fill-failure bullet is a pointer:** the edge-case bullet **A warm fill fails** points at §6.4 **Repository stores.**. Do not re-add causes there. [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
- **No literal `warming → failed`:** do not restore the edge in SPEC-2, the edge-case bullet, Decision 13, CODE-4 step 4, or TEST-1 item 18, because it is false on a preConnect pool. Do not name `sdk_connecting → failed` either, since §6.2 owns the per-path edges. Do not put a watchdog value or the §6.1 "measured from pod start" clock into SPEC-2. Re-anchoring the watchdog, rejecting preConnect plus gitRepository, capping the fill at the watchdog, and a minimum-timeout floor were all rejected. [spec.2.fix-G1.1, spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
- **Stale-snapshot watchdog findings:** a reviewer working from an old snapshot re-raises the preConnect watchdog finding. Check its quoted text against spec-changes.md **Repository stores.** before acting, because the finding was closed twice. [spec.2.fix-G1.1 (second round)]
- **TEST-1 item 18 has no `// spec:` line:** it matches its Tier 4/5 siblings (items 15-19), which rely on the blanket rule at the head of TEST-1. [spec.2.fix-G1.1]
- **Recycle re-warm tradeoff:** `sdkConnectTimeoutSeconds` also bounds the recycle re-warm leg, so raising it loosens re-warm hang detection. This is a deployer tradeoff and not a spec defect. [spec.3.review-performance.1]
- **No presence test on baseCommit:** a delta-built slot holds baseCommit's commit object in its own pack while its trees, blobs, and history stay store-only, so `cat-file -e <baseCommit>^{commit}` skips the fetch when the store changed. Always fetching, a closure walk, and copying the closure at capture were rejected. [spec.1.fix-G4.1, spec.1.review-reliability.1]
- **Rule 6 must rewrite storeCommit:** without the rewrite, the sequence S1 → S2 (delta) → re-checkpoint → restore onto S1 skips a needed fetch. TEST-1 item 11 "second restore onto the first store" discriminates. The rule-6 connectivity check excludes baseCommit's closure by construction, so it cannot catch the defect. [spec.1.fix-G4.1, spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
- **CODE-6 step 2.4 and storeCommit:** the step says "Index as CODE-5 step 6 states" and does not name the `lenny.storeCommit` rewrite. It relies on the heading's citation of §14 rule 6, and a non-spec round may want it named. [spec.1.fix-G4.1]
- **Runtime-removal edge case stays conditional:** pods whose store sits at the recorded commit restore with no RPC, so keep the edge-case wording conditional on "has to fetch". Plan-based authorization and dropping the Runtime check for SHA wants were rejected. [spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
- **Shallow-ancestry edge case with a store present:** the amend/reset edge case holds only when the ancestor is unreachable through the pod's store. With a store present and a shallow pack (delta over the bound), the store's history reaches the ancestor and the check passes. [spec.4.review-mechanism.1 (second round)]
- **Do not graft a shallow frontier:** do not fix the amend/reset failure by marking every commit with a missing parent as shallow, because that masks capture defects and makes TEST-1 item 11's missing-object row pass vacuously. Do not widen the rule 5 copy either, because it grows toward the rejected dissociate/repack non-goal. Expiring reflogs was also rejected. [spec.4.fix-G2.1, spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]
- **When the shallow restore occurs:** it needs a destination pod with no store for the url, or a delta over the §4.7.1 bound. A forward-refreshed store makes the delta empty. [spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]
- **Edge-case line 26 follows rule 6:** if rule 6 is reworded, recheck the shallow-restore edge-case bullet. [spec.4.review-single-source.1]
- **No `-c` overrides, no `safe.directory=*`:** a denylist is not fail-closed, and `-c` cannot neutralize `include.path`. Running git as the agent UID, Landlock (absent under gVisor), and rewriting slot config were also rejected. [spec.1.fix-G5.1, spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]
- **Every slot write is descriptor-relative:** a real-directory check followed by a path write loses to a live agent during the first capture copy. This includes the `lenny.storeCommit` rewrite, and the TEST-1 item 8 swap-race row discriminates. [spec.1.fix-G5.1]
- **A `.git` file is skipped:** a gitdir-pointer `.git` file, as a rule-4 worktree has, is skipped by the walk rather than failed. [spec.1.fix-G5.1]
- **Write files git reads into scratch:** GIT_DIR is a scratch copy, so `shallow` must be written there before `index-pack --strict` as well as into the slot. Without `shallow`, every shallow-fallback restore fails its connectivity check, and the TEST-1 item 11 shallow-fallback row catches it. [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1, spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]
- **Read-side bound lives in CODE-6 (2):** the §13.4 exception routes only writes through resolution. HEAD, refs, logs, and index reads are bounded by CODE-6 (2) copying regular files only. Trimming that step loses the read side. [spec.2.review-security.1]
- **Two 40-hex checks:** rule 1 (malformed `resolvedCommitSha`) and the §13.4 exception (malformed `lenny.baseCommit`) are different rules. Do not merge them. [spec.2.review-single-source.1]
- **Checkout needs `--force`:** after rule 3 removes `.git`, seeded or earlier-source files under `path` are untracked, and a non-forced `git checkout --detach` fails every 0109 derive of a cache-served session. Removing the whole subtree, widening §13.4 to rule 3, and failing on `path/.git` were rejected. [spec.4.fix-G2.1, spec.4.fix-design-G2.1 (second round)]
- **RuntimeAllow stays on the session Runtime:** only the CODE-5 step 1 url set comes from the pool Runtime. Do not unify them. Do not add a TEST-1 row for derived-on-base-pool, which is unreachable until the fallback lands. [spec.4.fix-G1.1, spec.4.fix-design-G1.1 (second round)]
- **"bind" in other senses:** binder, bindprepare.go, slotbinder.go, the CODE-5 "bind request", and pod-certificate binding stay. The 0109 Impacts row quotes 0109's "bind-time" restore and stays. Rule 1 no longer says "the Runtime the session binds to" (CORRECTS applied). [spec.4.fix-G3.1, spec.4.fix-G1.1 (second round)]
- **Qualify 0086 edits by SPEC number:** "0086 Edit (c)" is ambiguous, because several SPEC-n units have an Edit (c). [spec.1.fix-design-G6.1]
- **Quoted substring boundary:** 0111's §14.1 quoted substring must end before " (pinned", which is 0086 SPEC-2 (c)'s text. §26.3 bootstrap step 3 belongs to 0086 SPEC-6 (i), so do not stage it. [spec.1.fix-G6.1]
- **0086 glob trap:** `*spec-changes.md` also matches `non-spec-changes.md`. Select the `.spec-changes.md` file explicitly. [spec.2.review-citations.1, spec.4.review-citations.1]
- **"rm -rf" grep trips the spec hook:** a grep containing that literal against spec/ is treated as a write. Use the lookup tool. [spec.4.review-edit-sites.1]
- **§2 Non-Goals rewording:** the pod already GETs checkpoint chunks over its own network path, so any rewording that turns on "the pod's network path" re-scopes the object-store half. [spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
- **Do not re-file:** the §28 row for FetchGitPack, the §29 traces (they claim no completeness), the cold full-history mirror fetch (it matches gitref.Clone), the capture-copy alternates read (co-tenancy), the full-clone-fallback plan gap (Decision 8), copy cost (bounded by workspaceSizeLimitBytes), the claim-register rows (non-spec), and the two "Edge cases" sections (the non-spec one carries spoofed-podId and remote-deleted outcomes). Design-index rule-number citations are not copies. [various spec.4/spec.6 reviews]
- **Do not resolve the `FetchGitPack` pod through `podsession.Registry`:** the registry holds only the sessions its own replica coordinates, so a warming pod and a pod bound on another replica resolve to nothing. Rule P1 reads the `Sandbox` named `pod_id` (`spec.poolRef`, `spec.runtimeRef`) on every replica and reads `poolstore` only for the owner, because a CRD-only pool has no poolstore row. [redesign.3]
- **Do not restore the dev-path pod claim or an audience-only branch:** with an audience set, the TokenReview verifier and the cluster client are built in one `--agent-namespace` block, so taking the pod from the interceptor's unverified `kubernetes.io` claim serves no case rule P1 can resolve. One predicate, `podIdentityEnforced` (CODE-2 step 2), keys rules I1 and I2 and the rule 5 registration refusal, so the handler and the admin Router agree. [redesign.3]
- **Do not represcribe the shutdown means:** the single sender goroutine, `GracefulStop` with `Stop` after a fixed 5 seconds, and the "within 6 seconds" test bound are withdrawn. CODE-3 step 6 rules S1 to S4 are constraints, the bound comes from `--shutdown-timeout`, and the means is the implementor's. The earlier rejections of a bounded forced stop rested on harms rules S3 and S4 now forbid. [redesign.3]
- **Do not add a second gateway Token Service client or a VCS breaker:** `ReadCredentialSecret` is a `credassign.Client` method through `callTokenService`, sharing the one `token_service` breaker, and consumers answer the outage by CODE-7 step 7 rules O1 to O4. A separate client over `w.tokenServiceConn`, an informer resync interval, and the "no lease" §4.3 wording are withdrawn; summary decision 23 holds the reason. [redesign.3]

The §14 paragraph and its pointer and wording edits in §14, §14.1, and §26.2." [spec.1.fix-G6.1, spec.1.fix-design-G6.1]

- **D4 concepts.md Workspace sources** [non-spec-changes.md]: docs/getting-started/concepts.md **Workspace sources** says "The gateway performs the clone over its own network path". For a cache-served source the gateway fetches into its mirror and the adapter builds the repository from a store and a pack. No docs deliverable exists, and the non-spec loop should decide whether to stage a docs edit. [spec.1.fix-G6.1]
- **D5 docs pages** [non-spec-changes.md "Staged docs changes"]: the proposal stages no docs/ edit, although docs/ describes behavior it changes. docs/runtime-author-guide/runtime-configuration.md:110 says sharedAssets is a "List of `artifact` or `inline` entries", which leaves out gitRepository. docs/reference/metrics.md has no rows for lenny_workspace_git_pack_bytes or lenny_workspace_git_cache_sources_total, and its line 287 extraction-counter row does not record that workspace_size_limit is excluded. docs/getting-started/concepts.md:389 and docs/reference/workspace-plan.md:77 describe only the gateway clone, with no cache-served path and no workspace_size_limit failure for a tree that exceeds the pool's workspaceSizeLimitBytes. docs/reference/error-catalog.md has no UPLOAD_ARCHIVE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED row (pre-existing gap); decide whether the new reason needs one. docs/reference/adapter-contract.md needs a FetchGitPack row. schemas/lenny-adapter.proto has no FetchGitPack RPC yet (non-spec owns it). [spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1, spec.4.review-docs-alignment.1 (both rounds), spec.4.review-edit-sites.1]
- **D6 adapter git config and paths** [non-spec-changes.md]: if the spec fix lands, CODE-6 and the git invocation sentence must neutralize command-executing repository config and lstat-check the git dir and objects path. TEST-1 rows 10 and 11 need a symlinked `.git/objects` case and a `core.fsmonitor`-bearing repository case. The G5 design (scratch GIT_DIR, openat2 resolution, TEST-1 item 8 swap-race row) appears to cover the mechanism. No entry confirms the two test cases, so this item stays open until one does. [spec.1.review-security.1]
- **D7 single-flight lock scope** [non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 3]: CODE-3 step 3 says "Each operation on a url holds that url's single-flight lock". Read literally, every Pack stream (which can run to multiple GB), Ensure, and ValidateTree on one url is serialized per replica, which makes the per-url lock a hot-key bottleneck at Tier 3 and leaves `maxConcurrentPacks` (step 7) without effect for a single-repository pool. What should be true: the single-flight lock covers only mirror mutation (fetch, rebuild). Pack and ValidateTree run as concurrent readers, and the rebuild path removes the directory only when no reader holds it, or else readers retry once. [spec.4.review-performance.1]
- **D8 mirror capacity** [non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 steps 3 and 9, test 16]: "when a fetch fails for lack of space, the replica deletes that url's mirror directory ... and refetches once" and test 16 "a mirror volume at `sizeLimit` during `Ensure` rebuilds once and then fails with `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`" assume ENOSPC at the emptyDir sizeLimit. A disk-backed emptyDir with sizeLimit does not return ENOSPC. The kubelet evicts the pod once usage exceeds the limit (spec/04_system-components.md:273 states this for the workspace emptyDir). As staged (`git-mirror` emptyDir, sizeLimit 20Gi), mirror growth evicts a whole gateway replica and drops every session it coordinates. What should be true: the mirror self-enforces a byte budget below sizeLimit (measure before fetch, rebuild or evict urls), or the volume carries no sizeLimit-eviction cap. The new failure mode also needs an operator-docs narrative. [spec.6.review-reliability.1, spec.4.review-docs-alignment.1 (second round)]

## Ledger

### [spec.5.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: rule 3 builds the slot repository's git directory with `git init` in a fresh directory outside the build tree, runs every rule 3 git operation with that GIT_DIR and the source `path` as work tree, and moves it to `<path>/.git` after checkout, replacing any entry there — BECAUSE git then never opens repository state a plan source wrote, through any symlink at `path` or a parent component, and no new failure conflicts with the §14 last-writer-wins Path collision rule — ALTERNATIVES: the finding's suggested predicate (remove a symlink at `path`, fail on a symlinked parent) narrows round 4's cleanup one step and adds a failure; widening the SPEC-4 §13.4 exception to rule 3 copies refs/index/HEAD of a repository that is being created fresh; `-c` overrides are an open-ended denylist.
WATCHOUT: do not re-add a "remove the `.git` already at `path`" sentence beside the GIT_DIR ownership sentence; the removal is reachable around through an in-tree symlink an earlier source planted (symlink source under allowSymlinks, or the cold gitClone path's unconditional AllowSymlinks). Do not pass a work tree to `git init` either: it records an absolute `core.worktree` that points into the build directory after the move. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit (c) rule 3; non-spec-changes.md CODE-5 step 6; pkg/adapter/workspace/materialize.go writeSymlink (AllowSymlinks gate)
FACT: prototyped locally: `GIT_DIR=<tmp> git init; git config core.bare false; fetch; GIT_DIR=<tmp> GIT_WORK_TREE=build/src git checkout --force --detach <sha>` with `build/src -> evil` (evil/.git carrying a post-checkout hook and core.fsmonitor marker, plus an earlier same-path file), then `rm -rf build/src/.git; mv <tmp> build/src/.git`: no marker fired, the file held the pinned content, `git status` was clean, and no `core.worktree` was recorded. — EVIDENCE: shell prototype, this round
CORRECTS [standing context "Rule 3 `.git` removal" (spec.4.fix-G2.1 second round)]: rule 3 no longer removes a pre-existing `.git` before `git init`; it creates the git directory outside the build tree and moves it into `<path>/.git` after checkout. The §13.4 exception still stays scoped to rules 5 and 6, CODE-5 keeps `--force`, and TEST-1 item 8 now also covers a symlinked `path` and a symlinked parent component.
CORRECTS [standing trap "Checkout needs `--force`"]: the reason now reads "seeded or earlier-source files under `path` are untracked relative to the fresh adapter-created git directory"; the `--force` requirement is unchanged.

### [spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: §14 rule 3 builds the slot repository's git directory with `git init` in a new directory outside the build tree, runs every rule 3 git operation with that git directory and the source's `path` as the working tree, then moves it to `.git` at `path`, replacing any entry there. The "removes any `.git` entry ... without following a symlink" clause is deleted. BECAUSE git then never opens git state that a plan source wrote, whatever symlinks the build tree holds, and this is the CODE-6 bullet (2) per-operation-git-dir pattern applied to construction. It adds no new failure mode. ALTERNATIVES: the finding's own fix (remove a symlink at `path`, fail on a symlinked parent) narrows round 4's cleanup predicate the same way round 4 did, adds a failure that conflicts with §14 last-writer-wins, and still lets git run over build-tree state. Extending the §13.4 exception to rule 3 does not fit: its scratch copy of slot refs and index is the wrong tool for a fresh repository, and the build tree is not the slot. `-c` overrides of hook and fsmonitor keys are an open-ended list, and git still reads repo config (filters, includes).
CORRECTS [spec.4.fix-G2.1 (second round)] / Standing context "Rule 3 `.git` removal": removing the `.git` without following a symlink does not stop the attack, because a symlink at `path` or at a parent component reaches a planted repository that `git init` reinitializes and keeps (config and hooks survive). The settled line should instead record the external-git-dir construction.
WATCHOUT: `git --git-dir=X --work-tree=Y init` writes an absolute `core.worktree = Y` into config, and `GIT_DIR=X git init` alone sets `core.bare = true`. Construction must run `GIT_DIR=X git init` followed by `git config core.bare false`, or the moved repository points into the build directory after promotion. EVIDENCE: reproduced locally in /tmp (git 2.x on AL2023).
FACT: a local prototype ran external GIT_DIR init, fetch, `GIT_WORK_TREE=<symlink to dir holding a planted .git with post-checkout hook and core.fsmonitor>` checkout --force, `rm -rf path/.git`, and mv. The result left no marker, the pinned content won, status was clean, and there was no core.worktree. EVIDENCE: prototype only; nothing in tree.
FACT: the adapter build tree is `filepath.Dir(root)/staging` (mode 0755). For a root of `/workspace/current` it collides with upload staging and falls back to `current.build`, so `/workspace/staging/` (CODE-6 bullet (2)) is never inside the build tree. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/workspace/materialize.go promotionBuildDir, promotionStagingName, promotionStagingMode.
FACT: every existing source writer resolves `path` with resolvePath plus os.MkdirAll, which follow in-tree symlinks, so writing through an earlier source's symlink is the existing posture for all sources. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/workspace/materialize.go writeInlineFile, writeUploadFile, writeSymlink, resolvePath.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
FACT: `git ls-tree -r -t -l --full-tree <commit>` prints `-` for tree sizes and never lists the root tree; `git rev-list --objects --no-walk <commit>` lists the commit, root tree, subtrees, and blobs. A pack built from that listing indexes under `index-pack --strict` in an empty repo whose `.git/shallow` names the commit (git 2.50.1). — EVIDENCE: local reproduction in /tmp/g1t
WATCHOUT: `git rev-list --objects` prints `<oid> <path>`; piping it to `git cat-file --batch-check='%(objectsize)'` reports every named object `missing`, because cat-file splits the input line only when the format contains `%(rest)`. CODE-3 step 4 now uses `'%(objecttype) %(objectsize) %(rest)'`. — EVIDENCE: local reproduction; git-cat-file(1) `%(rest)`

Fix by adding `%(rest)` or `--no-object-names`. Not in G1's findings or design, so left for the mirror group or a later round.
DECISION: the §4.4 512Mi default for a zero `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` applies only at ValidateTree and restore completion (CODE-5 step 1), and restore completion reads the Resume request's `workspace_size_limit_bytes` — BECAUSE resolve.go's match also feeds the §7.3 Resume pre-extraction check, and defaulting there would make unset-pool sessions that checkpointed above 512Mi unresumable while the probe stays disabled — ALTERNATIVES: default in resolve.go (regresses resume); tree-wide default (filed in the summary defects list); treat 0 as unset (skips rule 2 and rule 6 size checks, not fail-closed).
FACT: no 512Mi constant exists in pkg/ or cmd/; resolve.go copies `WorkspaceSizeLimitBytes` only when the template sets it; the controller renders `--workspace-size-limit-bytes` only when set; `checkpoint.WorkspaceSizePreCheck` returns nil when limit <= 0; proto Resume field is `workspace_size_limit_bytes = 11`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resolve.go:158-163,352-353; pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:458-461,549-552; pkg/checkpoint/checkpoint.go:287-289; schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:1434-1440
FACT: docs/reference/configuration.md:116 already documents `pool.checkpointing.workspaceSizeLimitBytes` default 536870912 and "Must be > 0", so the code's 0-means-unset diverges from docs too (covered by the new summary defect bullet).

### [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: per-url mirror lock is shared for Ensure, ResolveRef, ValidateTree, and Pack; fetches for one url are serialized and identical ones coalesce; only capacity deletion takes the lock exclusively, via TryLock (CODE-3 step 3) — BECAUSE git fetch adds packs by atomic rename beside readers, so only directory deletion needs readers excluded, and a non-waiting writer cannot trigger sync.RWMutex writer preference — ALTERNATIVES: see WATCHOUTs below.
WATCHOUT: do not make fetches (Ensure, ResolveRef) take the url lock exclusively. Under Go's RWMutex writer preference a waiting Ensure for a new push queues behind a stalled Pack stream, and every later Pack and ValidateTree on that url queues behind it, which brings back the serialization. A blocking Lock in capacity deletion has the same defect and can also deadlock against Pack's nested have-Ensures. A reader-retry or reader-drain protocol was also rejected: it adds reader code and still fails in-flight streams. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 3; summary Decision 5 "single-flight per url" stays true because it is about fetches.
CORRECTS [Deferred D7]: closed. CODE-3 step 3 now uses the shared-lock and TryLock-deletion discipline, and TEST-1 item 20 asserts that Packs overlap, that a stalled Pack consumer delays neither a ValidateTree nor an Ensure, and that deletion skips the busy url.
DECISION: the git-mirror emptyDir carries no sizeLimit, and the gateway enforces `gateway.gitMirror.maxBytes` (default 20Gi) by LRU deletion of url mirrors it can TryLock, before each fetch. RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED means the mirror is over budget with nothing deletable left — BECAUSE a disk-backed emptyDir sizeLimit is enforced by eviction (spec/04_system-components.md §4.4.4 **Hard workspace size limit.** "if disk usage under the emptyDir volume exceeds the limit, the pod is evicted"), and /workspace/shared follows the same no-sizeLimit precedent — ALTERNATIVES: a sizeLimit backstop with maxBytes at 80% (two knobs, Helm cannot do quantity arithmetic, and an overshoot still evicts the replica); rebuilding the requested url (a fetch by SHA brings full history, so the rebuilt mirror is rarely smaller); deleting every idle url (causes a refetch storm).
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce an emptyDir sizeLimit "as a backstop" or any ENOSPC or "rebuild once" branch on the gateway mirror. The kubelet evicts the replica, which drops every session stream it holds, and the ENOSPC branch cannot be reached. TEST-1 item 22 pins that the sizeLimit is absent. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md §4.4.4; non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 steps 3 and 9.
CORRECTS [Deferred D8]: closed in CODE-3 steps 3, 6, and 9, in TEST-1 items 16 and 22, and in summary Decision 5. Its "operator-docs narrative" half is not closed here: a DOCS-1 row for the gateway.gitMirror values is a separate finding.
FACT: the extraction-abort metric is recorded only through Binder.gatedExtract → recordExtractionAbort → Binder.ExtractionAbort, so only a binder-level assertion modelled on TestBindUploadArchiveAbortRecordsMetric discriminates against a violation. podclaimerror never touches the metric. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/archiveextract.go recordExtractionAbort; binder.go ExtractionAbort field; binder_archive_test.go TestBindUploadArchiveAbortRecordsMetric
WATCHOUT: under §14 rule 3 one truncated delta pack succeeds, because the adapter retries with no have. TEST-1 item 16's truncation case must truncate every call, and the retry rows sit in item 6. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 rule 3 "requests the pack again with no have".
WATCHOUT: the malformed-object row (TEST-1 item 16) uses a `.git` tree entry because rule 2 does not refuse it. hasDotgit is WARN-level and becomes fatal only under the strict fsck that fetch.fsckObjects enables, so the row fails when the fsck config is dropped. Asserting "no ref" would pass vacuously, because a fetch by SHA creates no ref; assert that the commit object is absent instead.
DECISION: the chart test is appended as item 22 under a trailing "Tier 1 (Helm unittest):" heading, after item 21 — BECAUSE renumbering items 12 to 21 would break the item-number anchors of other groups — ALTERNATIVES: inserting it after item 11 and renumbering (rejected).

Do not drop the step 4 CEL rules on the strength of the new mirror check. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 steps 3 and 4
OPEN: the mirror check runs on the CRD's own list. An admin-derived Runtime whose base is a CRD is checked by validateSharedAssets at its own registration, but a later CRD edit to the base is not re-checked against derived Runtimes. Derived Runtimes cannot back a pool today (ResolvePool exact match), so no pod sees a merged duplicate; revisit when the §5.1 fallback lands.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G4.1]

DECISION: the CODE-4 warm fill runs inside `Server.ConnectGateway` (an unexported `gitstore.go` method called after the GatewayControl dial), with the deadline on a zero-defaulting `Server.GitStoreFillTimeout` and the url map on `Server` — BECAUSE cmd/lenny-adapter, cmd/runtimes/echo-embedded, and cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo all call ConnectGateway before they serve and log.Fatalf on its error, so one site covers every pod and the fatal-startup-error invariant (CODE-4 step 4) comes for free — ALTERNATIVES: an exported fill method called by each binary (three call sites; a binary that omits it reproduces the defect); embedded binaries rejecting gitRepository entries (contradicts SPEC-2's embedded sentence, and TEST-1 item 18's preConnect row runs on the embedded preconnect-echo); a new ConnectGateway parameter (signature churn; the Server-field pattern follows SessionStartAckTimeout and HeartbeatAckTimeout); adding the flag to the embedded binaries (that belongs to open question 4's rendering answer).
WATCHOUT: the embedded runtimes build from the root distroless Dockerfile, so once they run the fill their images need Dockerfile.git, which is why CODE-1 step 2 names them. Only TEST-1 item 18's echo-embedded SUCCESS row catches a missing git binary, because a failure row passes whether or not git is in the image. preconnect-echo has no release.yml entry, only build-images.sh and install.sh. — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/kind/install.sh:190,194; Makefile:50,210; .github/workflows/release.yml:115; scripts/cloud/aws/build-images.sh:59
FACT: echo-embedded and preconnect-echo call `sharedassets.Decode` and have no decoder of their own, so the old CODE-2 "cmd/runtimes decoders" target named nothing. — EVIDENCE: cmd/runtimes/echo-embedded/main.go:138; cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo/main.go:116
FACT: ConnectGateway's callers are cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:413, cmd/runtimes/echo-embedded/main.go:158, cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo/main.go:134, and pkg/adapter/gatewaylink_test.go. Its empty-gatewayAddr branch returns noopCloser today. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/gatewaylink.go:36-43
DECISION: the ENOSPC test is a tier-1 row in TEST-1 item 7 through the git-runner and filesystem seams. Its discriminating assertion is that nothing exists at destPath — BECAUSE a size-limited tmpfs needs mount privileges, and at tier 5 the kubelet evicts the pod and never returns ENOSPC to the adapter — ALTERNATIVES: tier-4 tmpfs, tier-5 disk fill (both rejected for those reasons).
FACT: G3's TEST-1 item 12 row already asserts that `sharedassets.Decode` keeps a gitRepository entry and `Materialize` writes nothing at its Path, so the design's conditional narrowing edit needed no change.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G5.1]
DECISION: The CODE-5 step 6 slot git directory is built in a fresh directory under the 0700 `/workspace/staging/` and set to mode 0755 before it is renamed to `<path>/.git` — BECAUSE promotion carries each entry's own mode onto the slot, the adapter (UID 65532) and the runtime (agentUID) differ on sidecar pods, and no agent-pod container may chown, so a 0700 `.git` leaves the runtime unable to run git. 0755 matches the cold path's extract.go `MkdirAll(..., 0o755)` — ALTERNATIVES: pinning every inner mode the way CODE-4 step 6 does for the store (goes beyond cold-path parity); a mode sentence in spec rule 3 (puts a code detail into the spec); a tier-1 mode assertion (one UID, so it asserts a constant).
WATCHOUT: CODE-6 bullet (2)'s mode-0700 git directory is per-operation scratch that is deleted afterwards, so 0700 is correct there. Do not "harmonize" it with CODE-5 step 6, and do not cite bullet (2) as the precedent for the promoted `.git` — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 (2); pkg/adapter/workspace/materialize.go promotionStagingMode comment.
FACT: `/workspace/staging` is created 0700 (pkg/adapter/warmlayout.go warmStagingMode), so the parent keeps the agent out while the slot `.git` is built. `git init` on an existing directory keeps that directory's mode. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/warmlayout.go:77-81,120.
FACT: only the tier-5 sidecar row (TEST-1 item 17) crosses the adapter/runtime UID boundary, so the runtime-UID `git status` check lives there.
DECISION: The concurrent-slot adapter case went into TEST-1 item 20, the -race row, as an extra sentence — BECAUSE a new item would renumber the citers of later items — ALTERNATIVES: adding os.MkdirTemp wording to CODE-5/CODE-6 (rejected: "fresh" already requires it, and the test pins it).
FACT: TEST-1 item 6 now covers all four adapter-guard conjuncts (non-40-hex sha, auth, depth, submodules: true) plus the nil-fetcher fail-closed arm. Item 10 asserts that either copy failing fails the checkpoint, and that a late commit lands in the slot's own pack (the discriminating case for the second copy).

### [non-spec.1.fix-G6.1]
DECISION: INFRA-1 step 3 is the single statement of the spike gate; step 4 now opens "When step 3 does not pass, implementation stops" — BECAUSE the old step 4 restated a divergent predicate (omitted (c), stopped on a slow checkout that step 3 lets pass as a recorded finding) — ALTERNATIVES: dropping step 3's checkout-cost escape (checkout cost is shared with the cold path (e), so it does not falsify the delta design); adding (c) to step 4 (two copies of one predicate drift again).
FACT: `lenny-test validate-maps` (tier 0) fails on any `_test.go` under tiers 2-12 that no tests/spec-map.json entry covers by file, `::Test`, or ancestor directory; build tags do not exempt a file, only `scaffolds_test.go` is exempt; tests/tier4_integration is mapped wholesale through a `tests/tier4_integration/...` entry, so tier-4 files pass without their own entry. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-test/cmd_validate.go validateTestFilesMapped and componentAndAboveTierDirs; tests/spec-map.json `tests/tier4_integration/...` entry.
DECISION: the spec-map obligation's one home is a sentence directly under non-spec-changes `## Testing` (covers INFRA-1 and TEST-1 and any CODE step landing a test); Files touched lists tests/spec-map.json; checklist S8 tiers became "0, 7a" — BECAUSE the TEST-1 preamble would not govern INFRA-1 — ALTERNATIVES: per-deliverable Target and summary index mentions (second homes).
WATCHOUT: the TEST-1 preamble's `// spec:` / `// diagnosis:` annotation sentence governs only TEST-1, yet INFRA-1's tier-7a spike is subject to the same tier-0 annotation predicate — EVIDENCE: cmd_validate.go componentAndAboveTierDirs feeds both predicates. Left as a separate finding by the G6 design; a later round can move it beside the spec-map sentence under `## Testing`.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
FACT: `git ls-tree -r -t -l --full-tree <sha>` omits the root tree and prints `-` as every tree's size. `git rev-list --objects --no-walk <sha>` lists the commit, the root tree, and every distinct tree and blob, and piping it to `git cat-file --batch-check='%(objecttype) %(objectsize)'` gives every size. A pack built from that listing indexes under `index-pack --strict` into a bare repository whose `shallow` file names the commit. Reproduced locally on 2026-10-30. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 steps 4-5
DECISION: ValidateTree keeps ls-tree for per-entry path, mode, and blob size (rev-list drops modes and lists a blob once even when it appears at several paths), and adds the rev-list|cat-file object listing for tree-object sizes and the memo's summed bytes. Pack step 5 pipes that same listing into pack-objects. — BECAUSE one listing feeds both the bound's tree term and the shallow pack — ALTERNATIVES: rev-list alone (loses modes, so the gitlink, non-regular, and symlink checks cannot run, and it undercounts duplicated blob paths); ls-tree plus `<sha>^{tree}` plus cat-file on tree oids (three commands, and the shallow pack still needs its own listing)
FACT: no code applies the §4.4 512Mi default for workspaceSizeLimitBytes. resolve.go leaves an unset template value at 0, the controller renders no `--workspace-size-limit-bytes`, nothing gives the slot volume a sizeLimit, and both `checkpoint.WorkspaceSizePreCheck` (the checkpoint probe and the §7.3 Resume pre-extraction check) and the proto comment on `workspace_size_limit_bytes` treat 0 as disabled. No 512Mi constant exists in pkg/ or cmd/. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resolve.go:158-163,352-353; pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:458-466; pkg/checkpoint/checkpoint.go:287-290; schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:1434-1440
DECISION: a zero limit becomes the §4.4 default (512Mi) only at the proposal's two new consumers, the binder's ValidateTree call and restore completion. The rule lives in CODE-5 step 1, and CODE-6 step 2.4 points at it. — BECAUSE applying the default in resolve.go's match also changes the existing §7.3 Resume pre-extraction check, the match's only other consumer (resume_rebind.go:264). With the checkpoint probe still disabled on unset pools, an ordinary session that checkpointed above 512Mi would then be unable to resume. — ALTERNATIVES: default in resolve.go's match (regresses non-cache-served resumes); implement the default across the tree in resolve.go, the controller flag, and the slot sizeLimit (the coherent fix, but a pre-existing defect outside this proposal's scope, so it is filed in the summary's defects list)
WATCHOUT: 0109 deliberately omits `workspace_size_limit_bytes` from FinalizeWorkspaceRequest, so restore completion called at 0109's restore seed (CODE-6 step 3) has no limit forwarded by the gateway. — EVIDENCE: proposals/0109_*/...summary.md:62
OPEN: on 0109's FinalizeWorkspace path, where does restore completion's rule 6 size limit come from? This is a separate finding against CODE-6 step 3 and is not fixed by G1.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: one per-url lock design closes the lock-scope and capacity findings together. Every mirror operation (Ensure, ResolveRef, ValidateTree, Pack) holds the url's lock shared. Fetches for one url run one at a time, and identical ones share one fetch. Only capacity deletion takes the lock exclusively, and only with a try-lock that skips a busy url. The ENOSPC "rebuild once" path is deleted outright — BECAUSE git fetch adds packs by atomic rename beside running readers, so deletion is the only mutation that needs readers excluded. A try-lock deletion never queues a writer, so a stalled Pack blocks nothing — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's RW lock with fetches exclusive was rejected because Go's sync.RWMutex blocks new readers once a writer waits. A stalled Pack would then block an Ensure for a newly pushed commit, which would block every later Pack and ValidateTree on that url, recreating the serialization. "Readers retry once after a rebuild" (D7's second option) was rejected because it adds a retry path to every reader.
DECISION: the git-mirror emptyDir carries no sizeLimit. The gateway enforces `gateway.gitMirror.maxBytes` (default 20Gi) before each fetch by deleting least-recently-used idle url mirrors, and fails the fetch with RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED when none is left to delete — BECAUSE a disk-backed emptyDir sizeLimit evicts the replica instead of returning ENOSPC (spec/04_system-components.md, the workspaceSizeLimitBytes sentence "the pod is evicted"). The `/workspace/shared` store volume already runs with no sizeLimit (pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/volumes.go), and the gateway container has no ephemeral-storage limit, so node storage bounds the overshoot — ALTERNATIVES: keeping sizeLimit as a backstop with maxBytes below it was rejected because it needs two knobs, a consistency rule Helm cannot compare across quantities, and it still evicts whenever an in-flight fetch overshoots. A gateway-side fixed fraction of sizeLimit was rejected as a hard-coded non-spec constant (code-best-practices.md, Configuration). Rebuilding the requested url when it alone is over budget was dropped, because a SHA fetch brings full history, so a rebuild rarely shrinks the mirror.
FACT: chart helm-unittest suites under charts/lenny/tests run at tier 1 through tests/tier1_unit/helm/helm_test.go (`helm unittest`), so a gateway-deployment_test.yaml row sits inside S11's tier set. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier1_unit/helm/helm_test.go:58
FACT: the extraction-abort metric is recorded only in Binder.gatedExtract → recordExtractionAbort → Binder.ExtractionAbort hook; podclaimerror never touches it. The binder precedent is TestBindUploadArchiveAbortRecordsMetric. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/archiveextract.go:163-199; binder_archive_test.go:185
FACT: gitref tests drive git against local path repositories rather than an HTTP fixture, and the reused fetch form sets GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0, so an auth-requiring fixture fails rather than hangs. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/provisioning/gitref/clone_test.go:25-64; clone.go:75
WATCHOUT: for the malformed-object test, `hasDotgit` (a tree entry named `.git`) is a WARN-level fsck message. It becomes fatal only because fetch.fsckObjects makes fetch-pack run index-pack with `--strict`. If the row ever passes without fsck enabled, switch to a tree with duplicate entries, which is ERROR-level under any strictness. Rule 2 does not refuse `.git` entries ("Uploads may write .git"), so fsck is the gateway-side guard here. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 3
WATCHOUT: under the try-lock deletion design a recursive RLock (Pack calling the public Ensure for unknown haves) cannot deadlock, because no writer ever waits. A fixer who changes deletion to a blocking Lock reintroduces both the deadlock and the stall propagation. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 5 "Unknown haves get one `Ensure` each"
OPEN: CODE-3 step 3's Ensure reuses `git fetch -- <url> <sha>`, which writes only FETCH_HEAD and creates no ref. Git's automatic gc after a fetch would treat every mirror object as unreachable: repack -A explodes them loose and later prunes them. A later round should decide between `gc.auto=0` on the mirror and a ref per ensured commit. Not filed by this group.
OPEN: DOCS-1 stages no operator-facing row for the `gateway.gitMirror.*` chart values (maxBytes, maxConcurrentPacks) or for the RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED mirror failure. That gap predates this design (D8's docs half), so it is a separate finding.
CORRECTS [Deferred D7]: closed by this design. The url lock is held shared by every operation, and only try-lock capacity deletion takes it exclusively, so Pack and ValidateTree overlap, and maxConcurrentPacks is the effective bound on a hot url.
CORRECTS [Deferred D8]: closed by this design. The volume carries no sizeLimit, the gateway enforces `gateway.gitMirror.maxBytes` with LRU deletion of idle url mirrors, and the handler's RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED row reads against that budget.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]

FACT: openapi.json's gateway entries are hand-authored. Its only go:generate step (genopsschemas) splices in lenny-ops paths and leaves the gateway components alone. The Runtime `sharedAssets` item has enum [artifact, inline] and `required: ["type","destPath"]`. generated_tools.go is derived from openapi.json, and `generated_tools_drift_test.go` fails when it is stale, so a test that pins openapi.json also pins the MCP tool schema. Nothing compares openapi.json with runtimestore.AllSharedAssetTypes. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/openapi.go:33-40; internal/genopsschemas/main.go:17-21; openapi.json:366-377; pkg/ops/mcp/generated_tools_drift_test.go:19-46
FACT: Runtime CRDs have no baseRuntime field, so the list on one CRD is that Runtime's resolved list. The CRD-path uniqueness check runs on the CRD's own `sharedAssets` and needs no merge. — EVIDENCE: pkg/apis/lenny/v1alpha1/runtime_types.go:140-150 (no BaseRuntime in RuntimeSpec)
FACT: there is precedent for a permanent rejection in `mirror` and for unit-testing it against `runtimestore.NewMemory()`, which makes it tier 1 rather than envtest: TestMirror_RejectsEmbeddedNonceOnly_spec_4_7. The encoder round trip has a tier-1 precedent in TestEncodeSharedAssets_RoundTripsThroughAdapterDecode_spec_6_4. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/runtime/controller_test.go:588; pkg/controller/sandbox/sharedassets_internal_test.go:14
FACT: gitref imports only pkg/workspaceplan, and runtimestore imports neither. The controller or runtimestore can therefore call gitref.NormalizeURL without an import cycle. — EVIDENCE: `go list -f '{{.Imports}}' ./pkg/gateway/provisioning/gitref`
FACT: cmd/runtimes/{preconnect-echo,echo-embedded} decode the flag with sharedassets.Decode and materialize through the same package, so the Materialize skip covers them. — EVIDENCE: cmd/runtimes/echo-embedded/main.go:138; cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo/main.go:116
DECISION: CRD-path url uniqueness goes in CODE-2 step 3 as one sentence. `mirror` applies the step 2 check to the CRD's list and returns a permanentError before mirroring, on the requireSoPeercred precedent. — BECAUSE the reconciler already has a pre-mirror permanent-rejection slot, and CEL cannot normalize. — ALTERNATIVES: a validating admission webhook for Runtime CRDs (none exists in the chart, and it is new surface); approximating normalization in CEL with lowerAscii and trimming (it duplicates the §6.4 rule outside its one Go implementation); dropping the uniqueness rule (it leaves CODE-4's url-keyed map ambiguous).
DECISION: all of CODE-2's test cases go into TEST-1 item 12, and the "Tier 2 (component):" heading (item 12 is its only item) is retitled to name tiers 1 to 3. — BECAUSE inserting new numbered items would renumber 13-21, which other designs and log entries cite by number. — ALTERNATIVES: a new tier-1 or tier-3 item (renumbers); a trailing item 22 out of tier order.
WATCHOUT: the CEL gitRef SHA rule stays the only CRD-time enforcer of per-entry rules. The new mirror check covers only uniqueness. Do not move the per-entry rules into mirror and drop CEL. — EVIDENCE: review-log Traps "The CEL rule is the only CRD enforcer"
OPEN: an admin-derived Runtime whose base is a CRD Runtime can gain a duplicate normalized url when the CRD base is later updated, because mirror checks only the CRD's own list. It is harmless today, because derived Runtimes cannot back a pool (ResolvePool exact match), and the binder and authorizer use the pool Runtime. Revisit when the §5.1 derived-to-base fallback lands.
USEFUL [review-log 225 UNVERIFIED, CRD-path uniqueness]: this entry is now resolved by this design.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: the warm fill runs inside `Server.ConnectGateway` (pkg/adapter/gatewaylink.go), which calls an unexported fill in gitstore.go after the GatewayControl dial; its empty-gatewayAddr branch returns an error when the shared-asset set has a gitRepository entry. The deadline is a new `Server.GitStoreFillTimeout` field (zero means the 15m default), set by cmd/lenny-adapter from `--git-store-fill-timeout`. The embedded runtimes leave it at the default and need no source edit. — BECAUSE all three entry points (lenny-adapter, echo-embedded, preconnect-echo) already call ConnectGateway and log.Fatalf on its error, so the fill reaches every pod with no per-binary call site. — ALTERNATIVES: an exported fill method that each binary calls (rejected: a binary that omits the call reproduces this exact defect); embedded binaries treating a gitRepository entry as a fatal startup error (rejected: it contradicts SPEC-2's embedded-mode sentence, which is spec-loop text, and it would let TEST-1 item 18's preConnect row pass on a rejection instead of a fill failure).
DECISION: CODE-1's git image set grows to lenny-gateway, lenny-adapter, runtimes/echo-embedded, and runtimes/preconnect-echo. TEST-1 item 18 gains an echo-embedded success row as the discriminator, because a failure row also passes when the image has no git.
DECISION: the volume-exhaustion test is a TEST-1 item 7 (tier 1) row. ENOSPC is injected through the git-runner and filesystem seams, and the row asserts nothing exists at destPath. A tier-4 tmpfs or loop device needs root, and at tier 5 ephemeral exhaustion evicts the pod instead of returning ENOSPC.
FACT: sharedassets.Decode is the only shared-asset decoder. The embedded binaries call it and have no decoder of their own, so the "cmd/runtimes decoders" in CODE-2 never existed as a separate site. — EVIDENCE: cmd/runtimes/echo-embedded/main.go:135, cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo/main.go:118, pkg/adapter/sharedassets/sharedassets.go:66
FACT: the embedded runtime images build from the root distroless Dockerfile. — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/kind/install.sh:190,194; Makefile:210 (echo-embedded-tarball); .github/workflows/release.yml:115; scripts/cloud/aws/build-images.sh:59
FACT: ConnectGateway callers are cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:413, cmd/runtimes/echo-embedded/main.go:153, cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo/main.go, and pkg/adapter/gatewaylink_test.go. No pod has a liveness or startup probe: only the sidecar adapter has a tcp readiness probe (podspec/sidecar.go:89). A long fill before Listen therefore delays readiness but does not kill the container.
WATCHOUT: Dockerfile.git must put the binary at the same PlatformBinaryPath, because the embedded container's preStop hook execs it. CODE-1 step 1's "builds the binary the same way ... same ENTRYPOINT" covers this, so do not loosen that wording. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/embedded.go (Lifecycle: preStopDrainHook comment)
OPEN: summary open question 4 says rendering adds a chart value, a controller flag, and an adapter argument. It omits that a rendered argument also lands on the embedded runtime container (the checkpointProbeArgs pattern, podspec/embedded.go:54), so echo-embedded and preconnect-echo would have to define the flag or flag.Parse exits. This omission predates G4 and is a separate finding.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]
DECISION: CODE-5 step 6 builds the slot git directory in "a fresh directory under `/workspace/staging/`" and sets it to 0755 before the rename; the "as CODE-6 bullet (2) does" pointer goes — BECAUSE CODE-6 (2)'s 0700 dir is per-operation scratch that is deleted, while step 6's dir becomes the session's `.git`, and promotion carries its own mode onto the slot (pkg/adapter/workspace/materialize.go promotionStagingMode comment) — ALTERNATIVES: recursive chmod 0755/0444 as CODE-4 step 6 does for stores (rejected: the slot repo is agent-written afterwards, and the cold path does no such thing; parity with the cold path's 0755 extracted dirs, extract.go TypeDir case, is the bar); `core.sharedRepository` (rejected: extra config surface for no gain).
FACT: adapter UID 65532 and agent UID 65533 differ on sidecar pods and share only the fsGroup cred-readers group — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go AdapterUID/AgentUID; base_pod.go FSGroup. Git creates children of the git dir under the adapter umask, as the cold path's MkdirAll does, so only the top directory needs the explicit mode.
OPEN: in sidecar mode every adapter-materialized workspace entry (cold gitClone included) is adapter-owned 0755, so whether the runtime UID can WRITE its `.git` (commit) at all is unverified and predates 0111. A later round should check before filing; do not fold it into CODE-5.
DECISION: discriminating test for the mode fix is a clause on TEST-1 item 17 (Tier 5, sidecar-model pool, real UID split): the runtime runs `git status` in a cache-served source. A Tier 1 mode assertion alone would pass under a single test UID without proving traversal.
DECISION: findings 6, 17, 15, 19 are test-list edits only (items 6, 10, 20); no CODE or spec text changes. Item 10's "a copy failure" becomes "a failure of either copy", matching rule 5's "either copy".
### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]
DECISION: CODE-5 step 6 drops "mode-0700" and the "as CODE-6 bullet (2) does" pointer, and chmods the git directory to 0755 before the rename into `<path>/.git` — BECAUSE the pod-level `/workspace/staging` is already 0700, so it keeps the agent out while the directory is built, and the promoted `.git` has to be traversable by the runtime UID the way the cold path's extracted `.git` is — ALTERNATIVES: pinning every inner directory and file mode (the cold path does not do this either, because extract.go MkdirAll 0o755 is umask-filtered, so this goes beyond parity); a new spec sentence in rule 3 (rule 3 states no modes, and this is an implementation detail).
FACT: pod-level `/workspace/staging` is 0700 (warmStagingMode), while the per-slot promotion build tree is 0755 (promotionStagingMode), and promotion carries each entry's own mode onto the slot. Adapter UID 65532 and agent UID 65533 are distinct. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/warmlayout.go:77-81; pkg/adapter/workspace/materialize.go:124-128; pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:52-53
WATCHOUT: CODE-6 bullet (2) keeps 0700 correctly, because its directory is deleted per operation and never promoted. Only CODE-5 step 6 promotes its directory. Do not "harmonize" the two. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 (2), CODE-5 step 6
UNVERIFIED: the runtime UID cannot write into adapter-owned 0755 workspace directories on the cold path either (the agent cannot commit to a cold `.git`). This is pre-existing, applies to the whole workspace, and is not caused by 0111. Whoever owns workspace writability in the sidecar model should check it. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/workspace/extract.go:59
DECISION: the runtime-UID test goes in TEST-1 item 17 (tier 5, sidecar-model pool), because tier-1 tests run as a single UID and `git status` passes there whatever the mode.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G6.1]
DECISION: INFRA-1 step 3 is the one statement of the spike outcome; step 4 becomes "When step 3 does not pass, implementation stops ..." — BECAUSE the two steps disagreed on (c) and on a slow (d); keeping step 3's (d) escape is right because checkout is paid by the cold path too ((e) times it), so a slow checkout does not falsify the cache, while (c) is the cache's whole value claim and must stop — ALTERNATIVES: drop the (d) escape so a slow checkout stops (rejected: it gates on a cost both paths share); fold the outcome into step 4 (rejected: step 3 already enumerates the conditions).
DECISION: the spec-map rule goes as one sentence directly under `## Testing` in non-spec-changes.md, ahead of INFRA-1, so it covers INFRA-1's spike, TEST-1, and any CODE step that lands a test — BECAUSE the TEST-1 preamble would not govern INFRA-1, and per-deliverable Target lines would need the same clause four times — ALTERNATIVES: TEST-1 preamble (reviewer's suggestion; misses the spike); adding tests/spec-map.json to every Target line (restates one rule per deliverable).
FACT: validateTestFilesMapped walks every _test.go under componentAndAboveTierDirs (tier2..tier12, including tier7a_load_local) with no build-tag exemption; tests/tier4_integration is mapped wholesale, tier7a and the rest per file. The same dir list feeds the tier-0 spec/diagnosis annotation predicate. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-test/cmd_validate.go:120-140, :709-793
OPEN: TEST-1's preamble requires `// spec:` and `// diagnosis:` only for TEST-1's tests, but INFRA-1's build-tagged spike under tests/tier7a_load_local is also subject to the tier-0 annotation predicate (cmd_validate.go:120-125). A later round may move that preamble sentence up under `## Testing` beside the spec-map sentence; not done here (separate finding).

### [non-spec.1.review-reliability.1]

FACT: a disk-backed emptyDir `sizeLimit` is enforced by kubelet eviction of the pod, not by ENOSPC; the spec says so for the slot volume ("if disk usage under the emptyDir volume exceeds the limit, the pod is evicted") and the summary relies on it (Decision 8 "evict the pod"). So CODE-3 step 3's "fetch fails for lack of space → delete url mirror, refetch once → RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED" never fires on the git-mirror volume CODE-3 step 9 sizes; the gateway replica is evicted instead, and TEST-1 item 16's mirror-full row cannot be produced. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:273; non-spec-changes.md:77, :94, :228; summary.md:25
WATCHOUT: the mirror has no shrink path other than that unreachable rebuild; every Ensure/ResolveRef only adds objects, so per-replica mirror growth is monotone over the replica's lifetime. Any fix needs an in-process byte budget below the volume sizeLimit (evict whole url mirrors under the write lock) rather than reacting to ENOSPC. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:74-77
WATCHOUT: CODE-3 step 3 makes every per-url operation hold one lock and "adds no reader-drain protocol", so the lock must cover the whole Pack stream; one slow FetchGitPack consumer then blocks Ensure/ValidateTree/Pack for that url on that replica up to the caller's deadline (15m warm fill vs 600s materialization). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:74, :77, :92, :104
FACT: agent pods are RestartPolicy Never (pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/base_pod.go:47) and the adapter is a regular container, so the warm-fill `.tmp-<hash>` → rename and the in-memory url map have no adapter-restart recovery case to handle. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/base_pod.go:47
